No wire change; the record stays 32 bytes, `Relay` 154 080 B. *Built 2026-10-15.* Tests (`routing.rs`): the failed neighbour that gets the message elsewhere and offers it back is now handed it again and goes on to deliver (`a_neighbour_whose_hand_over_failed_is_tried_again`, which on the commit before dropped it for want of a route); a split met by the search coming back merges at the receiver (`a_split_met_again_merges`); four tests whose receiver's ACKs were lost now expect that receiver to be offered the message once more and to return it (`a_split_copy_returned_to_a_relay_holding_none_searches_on`, `a_return_to_a_parent_that_gave_up_on_the_child_goes_on`, `a_return_does_not_go_back_and_forth`; `a_copy_with_a_count_already_seen_is_not_a_retransmission`, where the third copy now merges and nothing is dropped). Measured against D35 (`f20bad3`), same scenarios and lengths, paired seeds, differences ± one standard error. No message lost without a trace in any run, before or after. Mesh-burst at 64 times with threshold reception, seeds 1 to 100: delivered of what was taken, reachable, 0.9956 → 0.9964 (+0.0007 ± 0.0004, worst 0.827 → 0.857), messages lost for want of a route 1835 → 1560 (−2.8 ± 1.1 a run), 3541 → 3541 a run in the second half (+0.1 ± 2.6), airtime 2930 → 2909 s a run (−20.9 ± 9.8), hand-overs that succeeded 21 277 → 20 950 (forwards 21 147 → 20 847, returns 131 → 103), DATA frames 36 508 → 36 170 a run (−338 ± 166), failed hand-overs 1647 → 1647 a run, refused 9227 → 8647, drops NoRoute 2244 → 1747, ReturnFailed 276 → 244, hop cap 0 → 0, latency p50 / p90 / p99 / max 1.8 / 3.3 / 5.9 / 585 → 1.8 / 3.3 / 6.0 / 703 s. Hand-overs to a neighbour that had failed a hand-over of the same message at the same relay, D36's cost and benefit: 160 a run, 95 % taken. With the reception curve: 0.9994 → 0.9996 (+0.0002 ± 0.0001, worst 0.9942 → 0.9958), lost for want of a route 209 → 145 (−0.64 ± 0.21 a run), 3572 → 3573 (+1.0 ± 1.7), airtime 2691 → 2673 s (−18.4 ± 9.0), DATA frames −308 ± 153, failed 1899 → 1887, refused 147 → 105, NoRoute 337 → 218, ReturnFailed 43 → 25, latency 1.7 / 3.1 / 4.7 / 222 → 1.7 / 3.0 / 4.7 / 101 s; such hand-overs 73 a run, 86 % taken. The eight-relay ring at 1 s per leaf, seeds 1 to 30: 0.9871 → 0.9894 (+0.0022 ± 0.0008), lost for want of a route 611 → 415 (−6.5 ± 1.2 a run), 2213 → 2225 (+11.8 ± 13.1), airtime +2.3 ± 3.0 s; at 2 s 0.9962 → 0.9970 (+0.0008 ± 0.0002), 202 → 121 (−2.7 ± 0.6), 2473 → 2472 (−0.5 ± 10.2), airtime +4.0 ± 2.1 s; such hand-overs 58 and 25 a run, 89 and 96 % taken. The corridor at 1 s: 0.9592 → 0.9587 (−0.0004 ± 0.0013), 1273 → 1278 (+4.9 ± 5.7), airtime −1.0 ± 2.3 s, failed +4.8 ± 2.8 a run; at 0.5 s 0.9492 → 0.9502 (+0.0010 ± 0.0037), 1267 → 1272 (+5.0 ± 12.2), and more on the air: DATA frames +153 ± 53 a run of 11 451, airtime +10.8 ± 5.3 s, refused +118 ± 66; such hand-overs 11 a run there, 90 % taken, 197 of the 322 after a wait for a ban. Latency in the corridors and rings within the spread. S1, S1b, S2 and the corridor at their usual seeds: identical. Mesh-burst, seeds 1 and 5: all delivered, 238 → 236 hand-overs a run. The six-relay ring, seeds 1 to 5: 0.9964 → 0.9973 (+0.0009 ± 0.0005), lost for want of a route 24 → 12. Mesh-spread, seeds 1 and 5: the same delivery, but airtime 143.4 → 156.4 s a run (+13.0 ± 1.6), DATA frames 546 → 801, failed hand-overs 60 → 132 a run. All of it is one message a run, for a leaf of R18 while R18 reboots: R18 stays in its neighbours' tables and answers nothing, no relay owns the destination, and the search comes back through R18's neighbours again and again, three attempts at each neighbour that does not answer every time (seed 1: 95 such hand-overs, 9 taken). Before, one failure per record ended that. *Measured and not built:* marking a neighbour tried when it fails while already demoted. Mesh-spread +5.8 ± 0.2 s instead of +13.0, but in the threshold mesh +0.0003 ± 0.0002 instead of +0.0007 ± 0.0004 and the worst seed 0.818; the rest as D36. | D35 left one known hole (spec "Why the search finds a path"): a neighbour whose hand-over failed counted as tried. If it had never taken the message and later got it another way and offered it back, the sender took the offer for its return, the neighbour's own search ended, and the message was dropped where it could have been delivered. In the loaded corridor of D34, attempts lost to a collision at the receiver were four times as many as ACKs lost at the sender, so a failed hand-over mostly means the neighbour never had the message, and the old rule bet the wrong way. Only an ACK tells the sender the neighbour has the message, so only an ACK marks it. Measured, the loaded mesh and the rings lose fewer messages for want of a route and the mesh sends fewer frames; the price is a neighbour that does not answer being offered the message on every stay, which costs most where a search has no owner. Decided 2026-10-15. |
| D37 | **No queue of received frames** (`bark-driver`, `docs/firmware-adapter-2026-10-15.md` §2). The radio holds one frame; the loop reads it when it is next free, and a frame that ends while the previous one is unread replaces it. The loop must be back at the radio within 3.6 ms of any frame's end, the earliest the next frame's payload can start overwriting it (SX1261/2 DS §13.5, §7; a 0-byte frame at SF5 after a 12-symbol preamble). The core takes about 1 900 instructions per event, 7 600 at most, estimated at 0.5 ms on the nRF52840 at 64 MHz with the instruction cache off, plus 0.3 ms to read the frame over SPI. Every other task on the chip is kept short enough never to hold the processor past that; the firmware logs the longest time it was away from a listening radio (`longest_away_us`) every 10 s, and a board that exceeds 3.6 ms has a bug to fix, not a protocol to change. A frame lost this way is recovered as any frame lost on the air: DATA, ATTACH and DELIVER by the sender's attempts (spec "Hand-over"), an ACK by the sender's retry, which is answered again without acting twice (D35), a heartbeat by the next rotation. The simulator assumes exactly this: no processing time and no buffer. *Decided 2026-10-15.* | A queue filled by a higher-priority task would carry frames to the core late, and the core stamps a frame with the time it is handed over: a frame that waited would make an ACK look later than it was and put a busy reading in the wrong place (spec "Busy estimate"). It would need an arrival time in `Event::Received`, a contract change, and is only needed if later work (the Bluetooth link, a display refresh) cannot be kept short. If it must be, that work moves to a lower priority first. |
| D38 | **A next hop needs margin for a full frame through a fade** (spec "Neighbour table", "Next hop"; `radio.rs` `FULL_FRAME_ABOVE_SENSITIVITY_DB`, `FADE_DEPTH_DB`, `full_frame_rssi_above_noise_db`; `tables/neighbours.rs` `rssi`, `has_margin`, `frame_heard`; `relay/forward.rs` `next_hop`). The neighbour table keeps `rssi_ema`, which the code had never built: the averaged packet RSSI of every frame heard from the neighbour, heartbeats, its DATA and its ACKs, each new frame counting half, as the leaf's mean of two heartbeats (spec "Relay choice"). A neighbour has margin when it is at least 4.1 dB above the measured noise floor (spec "Busy estimate"). The derivation: a packet arrives only if every symbol does; with the symbol error rate of noncoherent detection of 32 orthogonal signals, anchored at the datasheet's 64-byte, 1 % point (the curve of `docs/reception-research-2026-10-15.md`; the 1.34 dB anchor shift cancels), a 255-byte frame (541 symbols) needs 0.44 dB more than the reference packet for 1 % loss; an 8-byte heartbeat (46 symbols) needs 0.46 dB less, and half of all heartbeats arrive 3.2 dB below the full frame's level. Sensitivity is 2.5 dB below the noise at SF5 (datasheet Table 6-1), so the full frame's level is −2.06 dB from the floor; with a fade of 4 dB, the standard deviation of the scenarios' fading over time and channel (a frame goes out on the neighbour's channel, whose fade this relay does not hear), the signal must average +1.94 dB; packet RSSI is signal plus noise (datasheet §13.5.3), so it reads 10 log10(1 + 10^0.194) = 4.1 dB. The 4 dB is not measured and is to be replaced in Phase 2. *The order of spec "Next hop":* margin first, then not demoted, then distance. A neighbour without margin is a last resort after every neighbour with margin, demoted ones included; it stays in the table. *Built 2026-10-15.* No wire change; the entry's 4 bytes fit its padding, `Relay` unchanged. Tests: `margin_follows_every_frame_heard` (table), `a_faint_neighbour_is_not_chosen` and `a_faint_neighbour_is_the_last_resort_and_its_ack_counts` (`routing.rs`; F, heard 2 dB above the noise, carries the message as the only way, and its ACK heard at −100 dBm gives it margin with no heartbeat between). Measured against `85bc49c`, before D39, paired seeds, differences ± one standard error; no message lost without a trace in any run. S4 (4500 s, seeds 1 and 2): at the spec "Capacity and sizing" texting rate delivered 0.492 → 0.871 (+0.379 ± 0.008), carried 2.37 → 4.21 texts/s, airtime per delivered text 4.63 → 2.45 s, DATA frames per hand-over that succeeded 4.14 → 2.63, relay time at the cap 31 % → 5.9 %, hand-overs per text 8.4 → 7.1; at half the rate 0.889 → 0.921 (+0.032 ± 0.015), 2.15 → 2.23 texts/s, 3.99 → 1.08 s, 5.16 → 2.07 frames, cap 5.5 % → 0.07 %. Without fading: at half the rate 0.9328 → 0.9336, airtime per text 0.87 → 0.79 s, frames 2.43 → 1.44, hand-overs per text 3.2 → 4.5; at the spec "Capacity and sizing" rate 0.9309 → 0.9282 (−0.0026 ± 0.0003), 1.03 → 1.13 s, frames 2.48 → 1.69, hand-overs 3.7 → 5.5: shadowing alone also puts weak links in the table, and the 4 dB is margin against a fade that is not there, so routes take shorter hops. The standard suite: S1 (seeds 1 to 20), S1b (1 to 100), the corridor and the ring (1 to 10) identical; S2 (1 to 20) 0.905 → 0.901 (−0.004 ± 0.014), airtime per text −0.021 ± 0.009 s, frames 1.51 → 1.11; its worst seed moved from 0.762 (seed 6) to 0.667 (seed 5, 21 texts: three more ran out of delivery time at their leaf's relay, none for want of a route); mesh-burst (1 to 10) 0.999 → 1.000 but hand-overs per text 2.1 → 5.2 and airtime +25.6 ± 4.0 s a run; mesh-spread (1 to 10) 0.989 → 0.988, hand-overs 2.0 → 4.7, airtime +17.1 ± 4.1 s. The loaded scenarios of D35 and D36 (30 seeds): the corridors at 1 and 0.5 s and the rings at 1 and 2 s identical, every link there has margin. **Mesh-burst at 64 times loses:** 0.9995 → 0.924 (−0.076 ± 0.026, worst seed 0.996 → 0.504), carried 2.42 → 2.00 texts/s, hand-overs per text 3.0 → 6.4, relay time at the cap 0.01 % → 11.7 %, airtime +1279 ± 100 s a run. Its 25 relays are 4 per km², without fading: the links between the full frame's level and 4 dB above it carry frames and are the long hops; without them each text takes twice the hand-overs and the bursts drive the relays to the cap. With 4 dB fading added to it the loss is smaller, 0.997 → 0.968 (−0.029 ± 0.010), hand-overs 3.8 → 6.1. *Measured and not built.* (1) Never a candidate: S4 at the spec "Capacity and sizing" rate 0.899, as the last resort; but mesh-burst 0.999 → 0.947 (−0.052 ± 0.015) and mesh-spread 0.989 → 0.941 (−0.048 ± 0.016) with no-route drops 0.1 → 6.4 and 0.9 → 6.7 a run, mesh-burst at 64 times 0.766 with 288 no-route drops a run: where the only way on is a weak link, leaving it out drops the message. (2) The average from heartbeats only: S4 at the spec "Capacity and sizing" rate 0.856, at half 0.919, within the spread of two seeds of the rule as built. (3) No fade depth, margin for the full frame's level only: S4 at the spec "Capacity and sizing" rate 0.714 (+0.222 ± 0.012), at half 0.913, airtime per text 3.78 and 2.53 s; mesh-burst at 64 times −0.009 ± 0.009, with fading −0.001 ± 0.002, the others within the spread. So the fade depth is what carries most of the gain in S4 and all of the loss in the sparse loaded mesh. *Measured again on D39* (`1592331`, S4, seeds 1 and 2), the rule as built and with no fade depth: at the spec "Capacity and sizing" rate delivered 0.943 → 0.949 (+0.006 ± 0.000) and 0.948 (+0.005 ± 0.001), airtime per delivered text 1.68 → 0.72 s and 1.22 s, DATA frames per hand-over that succeeded 3.7 → 1.5 and 2.8, demotions 58 000 → 4 400 and 31 000 a run; at half the rate 0.948 → 0.950 and 0.951, airtime per text 1.48 → 0.70 s and 1.14 s. With the keepalives gone the city delivers without the rule, and what the rule buys there is airtime: half as much per text, which is the headroom at the duty cap. *The leaf's choice of relay (spec "Relay choice")* is unchanged: a leaf already takes the strongest relay it hears and switches only for 8 dB, so a faint relay is chosen only when it is the only one, which is the last resort this rule makes for relays. | The capacity run found a text costing 4 to 6 DATA frames per hand-over at the spec "Capacity and sizing" rate, half of what was sent delivered, because fading lets an 8-byte heartbeat through on links that cannot carry a 121-byte frame, and greedy forwarding tries those first as they are the farthest. A heartbeat proves a neighbour alive, not that it can take a frame (spec "Neighbour table"); the margin asks the averaged signal to show that. Counting ACKs and DATA in the average lets a neighbour prove its link by carrying frames. A neighbour without margin stays a last resort, not excluded: D25's line has a demoted link as its only route, and the meshes show weak links that are the only route too; leaving them out lost messages for want of a route, keeping them as the last resort cost nothing there. The price is the sparse mesh at 64 times its load, where 4 dB of margin is too much for the density: a fixed threshold trades hop length for hop reliability the same way at every density. Ranking neighbours by expected progress per frame, the distance gained times the chance the link carries a full frame from the same curve and fade, would weigh the two; open. Decided 2026-10-15. |
| D39 | **The relay drives the keepalive, at a bounded cost** (spec "Hand-over", "Duty cycle cap", spec section "Attach and polls" (before D46), "Delivery queue", spec section "Leaf receive mode" (before D46), "Capacity and sizing", "Packet formats"; `tables/leaves.rs`, `relay/service.rs`, `node/leaf.rs`, `wire.rs`). (1) A leaf's ATTACH that attaches is answered with ATTACHED, type 0x7: the first byte, the leaf ID and the time from the end of the frame to the leaf's next poll in 10 ms units, 7 bytes, 10.56 ms; there is no ACK of an ATTACH. (2) The relay polls one leaf at a time on a grid of slots N = 300 s / 256 = 1.17 s apart (`POLL_INTERVAL`, the one constant to tune); each leaf owns one slot of the 256-slot cycle and is polled every P = 256 N = 300 s. A poll is an ATTACHED; it goes after an owed reply and before the heartbeat and the hand-over, never while the relay waits for an ACK, and is skipped if it cannot start within 500 ms of its slot. (3) The leaf answers each poll with one ATTACH whose aux bit 3 marks it as a reply, which is not answered, and sends an ATTACH that attaches only at the start, after giving its relay up, or after choosing another; it follows its relay to a new home channel without attaching again. (4) The relay drops a leaf after k = ⌈TTL / P⌉ + 1 = 3 unanswered polls, once the last answer is overdue, 0.6 s after its slot; the leaf gives its relay up after k empty windows, or after hand-overs failed in two 60 s periods in a row. Queued messages of a dropped leaf stay until their TTL, as before. *Superseded by D41.* | Keepalives were the largest load in the domain: 66 leaves' ACKs took 14 % of a relay's duty budget, and ATTACHes and ACKs 77 to 83 % of a channel summed over the domain (`capacity-research-2026-10-15.md`). Polls cost the relay at most 10.56 ms per N whatever its table holds: 0.90 % of its time, 9.2 % of its budget at a full table, 2.9 % at 70 leaves, measured 2.9 %. N follows from the budget and the TTL: a full table must leave forwarding nine tenths of the budget, so N ≥ 1.07 s, P ≥ 275 s; P is half the 600 s TTL, so with k = 3 a phone dark for less than a TTL keeps its relay, and a gone leaf is kept at most a period past the life of its messages. A sleeping leaf (spec section "Leaf receive mode" (before D46)) hears only a 512 ms preamble, which would make each poll 49 times longer; instead it wakes for its slot with the ordinary receiver. Its window is the slot less and plus the drift of two ±50 ppm crystals since the last ATTACHED (30 ms after one period, 90 ms after three) and 5 ms of rounding, to 500 ms of lateness and the 10.56 ms frame after it: 0.58 s every 5 minutes, 11 µA. The lateness bound: a hand-over under way, 238.6 ms, an owed ACK, 114.4 ms, and channel checks. The nRF52840's internal RC oscillator, ±500 ppm, on which the firmware's clock runs today, would widen the window to 1.1 s. ATTACHED does not name the relay; a leaf takes a poll only in its window, so another relay's poll on the same channel is ignored. A lost ATTACHED or ATTACH costs one round. Fixed slots rather than a rotation of L N for L leaves, so that a leaf that misses a poll knows when the next comes; with fewer leaves the relay polls less, not faster. Measured on S4 against A, the 60 s keepalive (note removed 2026-10-18; in git history): at the spec "Capacity and sizing" rate delivery 52.5 → 94.6 % and 45.9 → 94.1 % (seeds 1, 2), carried 2.5 → 4.6 texts/s, centre relays at the cap 57 → 0.2 % and 66 → 2.6 % of the time; at half the rate 90.5 → 95.0 % and 87.3 → 94.6 %; leaves without a relay at the end 6 000 → 400; DATA frames per hand-over 4.1 → 3.6, so part of what the capacity note put down to fading was collisions with keepalives. Every relay on a roof: 11.6 → 94.5 % at a tenth of the rate; at the spec "Capacity and sizing" rate 0.0 → 7.2 %, now from DATA load, with 46 000 polls skipped on busy channels. Worse: a phone back from the dark is heard at the median after 180 s instead of 60 s (its messages still come with the 60 s retry); a dead relay and a moved leaf are noticed after up to 15 minutes instead of 6.5 and 3. S1, S1b, corridor, ring and meshes unchanged within noise, S2 +3.3 ± 0.6 points over 60 seeds. Later, not built: a sleeping leaf's poll window could also carry its DELIVER with the short preamble, at up to 5 minutes more latency. |
| D40 | **Transmit power control: measured and not adopted; the prototype is removed** (spec "Heartbeat", "Capacity and sizing", O8; `docs/power-research-2026-10-15.md`). Every frame goes at `Config::tx_power_dbm`, and a heartbeat's five spare position bits are sent as zero and ignored. *Measured* on S4 and its variants against main `2097d3a` (D38 and D39 in), seeds 1 and 2, delivered of the texts sent from 900 to 3900 s: (a) one operating power for every frame, the lowest at which N neighbours hear a full frame, at twice the spec "Capacity and sizing" rate 37 to 59 % against 94 % at full power, and with N = 8 one relay cut off in each seed; N had to be 12 before the relays stayed connected. (b) Heartbeats at full power and every other frame at the power its receiver's measured loss needs, at twice the rate 94.6 against 94.4 % (seed 1) and 94.5 against 94.0 % (seed 2): a third less area disturbed by each frame, and no change, since the channels were not what bound. (c) Heartbeats at the operating power of (a), one rotation in three at full power, and data as in (b): with every relay on a roof and all hearing each other, at the spec "Capacity and sizing" rate, 7.9 % at full power, 12.9 % with (b), 90.5 to 90.8 % with (c) at N of 8 to 16; in S4 at twice the rate, N of 12 or 16, 92.7 to 94.4 % in seed 1 but 77.5 to 81 % in seed 2 against 94 %, and 52 to 91 % with N = 8, because the rooftop relays that carry the long hops are turned down. In overload, a 2 × 2 tiling of domains whose texts cross all 112 km² at the spec "Capacity and sizing" rate, full power carries 4.7 texts a second, (b) 2.1 and (c) at N = 12 1.6. | The design is sized on S4, where a relay's 10 % duty cycle binds before the channel does (`capacity-research-2026-10-15.md`). Power cannot change the duty budget: shorter hops cost the same airtime a hop and there are more of them, so (a) costs capacity there and (b) buys nothing. Power control helps only where the channel binds, in a domain of rooftop relays that all hear each other, and the rule that helps there, (c), costs the mixed domain the design targets. A relay cannot yet tell which of the two it is in, and no gate for it was measured. The research note recorded the prototype behind a switch, off by default; code that was measured and rejected is removed rather than kept off, and the spare bits, with nothing left to read them, go back to zero. Spec "Capacity and sizing"'s "+ per-packet power control ≈ 2× capacity" assumed the channel binds; the doubling it expected came from D38 and D39 instead. Decided 2026-10-16. |
| D41 | **The relay polls every 300 s with its leaves' slots spread, and a leaf keeps its relay while it is dark** (spec section "Attach and polls" (before D46), spec section "Leaf receive mode" (before D46), "Capacity and sizing", "Packet formats"; `tables/leaves.rs`, `relay/service.rs`, `node/leaf.rs`, `wire.rs`; (note removed 2026-10-18; in git history)). (1) The relay polls and the leaf answers right behind the poll, as in D39; the relay then listens for the answer as for an ACK, up to the ACK timeout, sending only an owed reply. ATTACHED's `next_poll` is the delay to the leaf's next slot, not a period. (2) Each leaf is polled every P = 300 s, as in D39, and with L leaves its next slot is the first time a period after its last that lies P / L from every other leaf's, never under a full table's 1.17 s, so every slot of the round is used. A leaf is dropped once its polls have gone unanswered for a TTL. (3) ATTACHED stays 7 bytes; its four `aux` bits count the messages waiting for the leaf, up to 15. A sleeping leaf that answers a poll listens 6.12 s + n × 3.13 s for them, and the relay sends those with the short preamble (spec section "Leaf receive mode" (before D46); relay side built, the leaf core runs only the listening mode). (4) A leaf whose window passes empty takes the next poll whenever it comes and attaches again a random 0 to 60 s after the next heartbeat it hears, or a rotation later if none: to its relay if heard since the polls stopped, else to the strongest other heard since. It gives its relay up after failed hand-overs only if it has heard another since the first. *Measured* against main `b86c3f5` (S4, seeds 1 and 2, more where said): at the spec "Capacity and sizing" rate delivered 95.1 → 97.0 % and 94.7 → 96.9 %, total airtime +4 %, a phone back from the dark heard after about 55 s at the median instead of 175 s (90th percentile about 200 s instead of 289 s), leaves without a relay at the end 405 → 200, keepalives 2.9 → 2.8 % of a relay's budget; the busiest minute of five holds 22 % of a relay's polls instead of 53 to 55 %, the domain's polls a minute about 4 300 instead of 940 to 12 600, after a mass start and after 27 relays restart. At twice the rate, four seeds: main 94.4, 94.0, 91.9, 49.5 %, D41 92.9, 78.8, 84.3, 46.2 % (−6.9 ± 3.1 points paired): twice the rate is the cliff's edge for main too, and D41's 5 % more attached leaves and its ATTACHes after a missed poll tip more seeds. Small cells against main: S2 +1.8 ± 0.6 points (60 seeds), the rest within noise, airtime the same. The figures from three times the rate on, below, were taken with the period shortened as below, which ran short in under 7 % of relay-minutes there; the restart run without it delivered 96.5 %. At three times both collapse in the same bin (22.1 → 20.7 %, 10.7 → 11.9 %); all on roofs 95.0 → 96.0 % at a tenth of the rate, 7.9 → 7.3 % at the spec "Capacity and sizing" rate; 27 relays restarting 93.8 → 96.3 %. Standard and loaded suites within noise, no message lost without a trace outside the restart runs (main loses as many there). *Departures from the design as decided:* ATTACHED was to be 8 bytes with a byte counting; the byte makes it 11.84 ms instead of 10.56 ms, and on main alone it took twice the rate from 94.4 and 94.0 % to 93.5 and 91.6 %. A bit saying the relay has room was to end a refused leaf's hold; the room of the instant a relay polls holds at most polls of a relay at its cap, and the leaf told so skips the doubling hold that spaces refused offers: −0.9 ± 0.6 points and 5 % fewer texts carried on the ring at its cap, −0.3 and −0.7 points for a no-room poll counted as a refusal; left out. The answer wait was not in D39: without it a relay's hand-over queued behind a poll made it deaf to the answer, and on the ring, whose texts repeat in step with the period, a relay dropped a leaf that was there (−0.8 ± 0.5 points, 5 % fewer carried). *Built, measured and removed:* a period of L × 4 s within 10 s and 300 s while the relay's channel read quiet (3.5 to 4.5 % busy) tripled S2's airtime (74 → 219 s a run), cost the rings at their cap 2.5 % of their carried texts and corridor seed 3 0.99 → 0.89, and gained nothing at the spec "Capacity and sizing" rate (96.6 and 96.4 % against 97.0 and 96.9 % without it); shorter periods for small cells wait for sleeping leaves and a measurement of the latency they need. The gain at the spec "Capacity and sizing" rate comes from the leaf's quick return, not from polling more often. | The collapse note found D39's polls bunched: leaves attached together took consecutive slots of the fixed grid, and every relay polled most of them in one minute of five, 28 % more airtime in the domain then. Spacing every leaf's slot from the others' spreads them by construction, with every slot used. The relay drives because the leaf-driven attempt, ATTACHes at times the relay named, collided with DATA the leaf could not hear and collapsed at twice the rate (66 and 64 %; 28 % of lost DATA had an ATTACH or ATTACHED on the air against 8 % on main). A leaf keeps its relay while dark because a phone in a pocket hears no relay at all, and should find its relay and its messages when it is back; the heartbeat gate and the random minute keep a dark phone to one ATTACH a rotation and spread a trainful surfacing at once to 5 ATTACHes a second for 300 phones. The period stays at D39's 300 s because nothing measured gains from a shorter one. Decided 2026-10-16. *Superseded by D46 (one node type, no polls, no leaves).* |
| D43 | **The channel plan stays at 13 × 125 kHz at 10 mW; the band's 1 mW row is not used** (spec "Regulatory limits", "Channel plan"; `docs/bandwidth-research-2026-10-16.md`). The duty cycle is per device over the whole band (ERC/REC 70-03 general part; EN 300 220-1 §5.4.1, -2 §4.4.3.2), so more channels buy no budget; wider channels do, as shorter frames: measured on S4 (two seeds, main `f1fa059`), 250 and 500 kHz cut airtime per text 0.72 → 0.34 → 0.16 s and the busiest relay's airtime at twice the rate 8.4 → 4.1 → 1.7 %, with delivery unchanged at one and two times the rate and the collapse at three times unmoved (the channel's busy share is the same at any width, 1.4 % at the spec "Capacity and sizing" rate). Their cost is 3 and 6 dB of sensitivity, unmeasured where it matters, the indoor leaf link; leaves changing relay 8 000 → 15 400 → 28 700 times a run; and at 500 kHz three channels that cannot avoid 433.92 MHz. The 1 mW row disconnects S4 at its relay density (26 % delivered); with 3.6 times the relays it delivers as 10 mW does at one and two times the rate with no less airtime per text, and collapses at four times with no cap to hit, since the cap is also a collision brake. | Not a capacity lever where the channel binds, and the sensitivity side of the trade is a bench measurement. Revisit after Phase 2 measures the loss curve and the wall loss, and after D42 sets the cliff. Decided 2026-10-16. |
| D44 | **A message ends when its time does** (spec section "Time to live" (before D113), spec "Overload waits", "Return to parent", "Bounds", spec section "Leaves" (before D46), spec section "Attach and polls" (before D46), "Delivery queue", "Packet formats"; `wire.rs` `TTL_UNIT`, `node/relay/forward.rs` `expire`, `node/leaf.rs`; `docs/ttl-2026-10-16.md`). (1) DATA carries one more byte, the time the message has left of the 600 s delivery TTL in whole units of 2.5 s, rounded down; the header grows to 22 bytes, the payload shrinks to 233 so a full frame stays 255, and a DATA without payload stays 18.24 ms. (2) The leaf stamps the TTL less the time it has held the message, a relay sending for its own application starts at the TTL, and a relay counts a received message's time from the packet less its time on air; every attempt carries what is left then. (3) With one unit or less left a message is dropped (`Expired`) wherever it waits in the send queue, at once, and a packet that arrives so is taken as no work and ends; this adds an end to the list of D31. At its owner the delivery queue's own TTL takes over, so a whole life is bounded by two TTLs. *Built 2026-10-16* (`e132caa`, `4cd7673`, `a950e64`, `d170923`). `Relay` +2360 B, one instant per send-queue place. *Measured* against main `bddfb50`, S4 seeds 1 and 2, delivered of the reachable texts sent from 900 to 3900 s, main → TTL: ×1 96.7 → 96.6 % (−0.08 ± 0.06), ×2 95.8 → 96.0 % (+0.16 ± 0.03), ×3 31.3 → **40.4 %** (+9.1 ± 0.7), ×4 9.9 → **15.8 %** (+6.0 ± 0.2); texts carried a second at ×3 4.40 → 5.71 and at ×4 1.74 → 2.81; airtime −0.7, −3.9, −5.8 and −2.8 %; relay time at the cap at ×3 54 → 48 %. The cliff is in the same bin; after it delivery settles: at ×3, from 2400 s, 18 to 29 % of each 600 s bin, where main falls from 19 to 4 %. Longest life in the network 2992 → 1194 s at ×3, oldest in flight at the end 3192 → 1171 s. Messages ended by time: 1 and 3.5 a run at ×1 and ×2, all reaching a relay in their last seconds; 4883 and 9850 at ×3 and ×4, three quarters of them in hop places, none as a return. Loaded suites (corridor at 1 and 0.5 s, ring at 1 and 2 s, 30 seeds) and S1, S1b, S2, the corridor, the ring and mesh-burst: identical summaries; mesh-spread −0.11 ± 0.11 points (one message of 122 hand-overs that rounding gave less time), mesh-burst ×64 −1.0 ± 0.7 (30 seeds). S1 with B removed: identical, its message ends at the neighbour's expiry, 413 s, before its time. *The unit:* rounding down loses a whole unit at a hand-over that follows at once, so the unit also caps the hand-overs a message has time for: 239 at 2.5 s, 59 at the draft's 10 s. Measured, 10 s ends searches that would reach their owner (mesh-spread −1.24 ± 0.11 points, 64 messages a run at S4 ×2) and gains slightly more past the cliff (+10.0 and +6.5 points); two bytes of 10 ms keep the time exactly and gain +4.6 and +5.0, for a byte on every frame. | Past the cliff messages lived up to an hour in the network and about 1500 frames each, long after their leaf and their destination's queue would have given them up, and kept the relays at their cap (collapse note, D42); the backoff of D42 makes them wait and does not end them. The TTL is the one the leaf and the delivery queue already keep. Kept as built, one byte of 2.5 s: the draft's 10 s ends searches by their hand-overs, and two bytes give up half the gain for a byte on every frame. First set aside on 2026-10-16 as not worth a byte per frame for a domain past its cliff, then adopted the same day: a domain at three times its rate delivering a fifth to two fifths instead of nothing is what matters in a disaster, which is when a domain is at that load. The owner's delivery queue keeps its own 600 s and does not count against the message's time: a message held there has arrived as far as the network is concerned and costs it nothing more; the same holds for any hold at a destination's last hop. Decided 2026-10-16. |
| D45 | **A relay does not refuse fresh work for its own backlog: measured and not adopted** (`docs/admission-2026-10-16.md`). What was measured, G1: Fresh work, a leaf's DATA or the relay's own application's message, is refused, as for a full room, while every live neighbour is banned (D34, now its special case) or while a forward or return the relay holds waits for a ban, after a refusal or silence, or holds a hop place. A message queued in the regular room waits only for the hand-overs ahead of it, and a DELIVER for its leaf; neither counts. No new constant, no wire change. *Built 2026-10-16* on a research branch, not merged. *Measured* against main `8cd70a4`, S4 at 125 kHz, seeds 1 and 2, delivered of the reachable texts sent from 900 to 3900 s, main → this rule (G1) → a failure gate (G2: no fresh work for `hold(k)` after the k-th failed or refused hand-over in a row) → the whole-relay hold (H): ×1 96.65 → 96.71 → 96.66 → 96.71 %, ×2 95.96 → 95.95 → 95.87 → 96.02 %, ×3 36.5 → 37.5 → 35.0 → 41.2 %, ×4 10.0 → 10.5 → 10.4 → 10.3 %; G1 − main ×3 +1.1 ± 2.9, ×4 +0.5 ± 1.3 points. The cost of G1 at the spec "Capacity and sizing" rate: +0.07 ± 0.02 DATA frames per text, +0.4 % airtime, latency unchanged; H adds +4.0 ± 1.5 s at p90 there and +12.2 ± 1.6 s at twice it. Loaded suites, 30 seeds, G1: the ring at 1 s and 2 s carries 2.506 → 2.626 and 2.752 → 2.813 texts a second, mesh-burst ×64 delivers 0.946 → 0.970 (+0.024 ± 0.011) with 132 ± 50 s less airtime, the corridors repeat main; the standard suite unchanged but for the ring at 10 seeds, 0.9987 → 0.9971 (−0.0016 ± 0.0015), one seed congested at the end of its run. Lifetimes with G1 alone at ×3 and ×4: the longest in the network 46 and 59 minutes, 22 % and 32 % of the admitted messages past 600 s; with the TTL of D44 merged in as well, delivered 39.3 and 16.2 % against 40.9 and 16.8 % with the TTL alone, the longest life 1196 s. Throw-away: an ACK bit saying the receiver takes no fresh work, which the sender obeys for `hold(k)` and repeats while its own gate is closed, reopened by a plain ACK, gave S4 ×3 46.8 % and ×4 21.3 %, relays at the cap 5 % of the time against 50 %, the longest life 29 minutes, but cut what the loaded corridor and ring carry to a quarter to an eighth; set on the relay's own evidence only it is safe and carries a little more than G1 in the loaded suite, and gives S4 ×3 37.9 %, ×4 11.8 %. | The collapse note found the hand-overs into the centre fail first; D42 slows the sender of a failed hand-over, but the edge admits everything its bucket can start. Of D34's argument, that overload is better felt at the origin than held in a relay that cannot send it, waiting for a ban is the case D34 covered only when every way on was banned. Measured, the rule is free at the design rate and helps where a relay's own neighbour is the bottleneck (the loaded rings, mesh-burst ×64), but not in S4: there the relays near the centre already refuse for their bucket (400 000 refusals a run against 2400 more from the gate), and the edge relays whose texts clog the centre see no trouble of their own, so pushback at the edge needs a signal carried back from the centre, and one that cannot keep itself up around a loop; none such was measured. It is coarse: one message waiting for one neighbour, for example for a leaf's full delivery queue, closes the relay to fresh work for every destination until it moves. Only a time bound shortened the lives, which is what D44 does. G2 does as well in the rings and less in mesh-burst ×64 with a count and a timer; H buys its S4 gain with latency at the design rate. Not adopted 2026-10-16: the gain where a relay's own neighbour is the bottleneck does not pay for closing a relay to every destination behind one stuck message, and in S4, the design's domain, it does nothing. What remains open is a signal carried back from the centre that cannot keep itself up around a loop. |
| D42 | **Silence is pushback: an unanswered hand-over bans the neighbour, and the message waits for it** (spec "Neighbour table", "Message records", "Next hop", "Return to parent", "Hand-over" step 6 and "Bans", spec "Duty cycle cap", "Bounds"; `tables/neighbours.rs` `failed`, `node/relay/forward.rs` `wait_for`; `docs/backoff-2026-10-16.md`). (1) Three unanswered attempts ban the neighbour for every message, as a refusal does, for one ban doubled for each earlier failure in a row, 1.56, 3.11, 6.2 s and so on up to the bucket's refill of 61 s, the ladder spec "Hand-over" already uses for a relay's own refused message (`budget::hold`); an ACK resets the failure count and so the ladder. Messages whose every candidate is banned wait, most travelled first when the bans end, and fresh work is refused while every live neighbour is banned (D34). (2) The failed message waits for that neighbour's ban and is offered to it again with three fresh attempts; it goes to the next candidate only once the neighbour is demoted, after the second failure in a row (spec "Neighbour table"). A return waits for the parent the same way and is dropped only then. Refusals are unchanged. No wire change, no new constant. *Built 2026-10-16* (`b750744`, `ab91bb9`, `32e759c`; instrument `9ba6e17`; going to the next candidate at once instead was measured behind a switch and taken out, `239027e`). Measured against main `b86c3f5`, S4 seeds 1 and 2, delivered of the reachable texts sent from 900 to 3900 s, main → reroute → wait: 125 kHz ×1 94.9 → 95.2 → 95.2 %, ×2 94.2 → 91.3 → 94.2 %, ×3 16.4 → 15.8 → **32.9 %**, ×4 2.4 → 2.3 → **8.0 %**; 250 kHz ×1 94.9 → 95.1 → 94.9 %, ×2 94.9 → 94.8 → 94.6 %, ×3 14.1 → 13.4 → **82.6 %**, ×4 1.8 → 0.0 → **11.3 %**. At 250 kHz ×3 DATA frames per text 38.7 → 39.1 → 17.9, relay time at the cap 66 → 66 → 15 %. The cost at the spec "Capacity and sizing" rate: +0.43 ± 0.11 DATA frames per text, +2.7 % airtime. Standard and loaded suites, wait against main: S1b and the corridor 0.960 → 1.000 (60 seeds), the corridor at 1 s and 0.5 s per text 0.950 → 0.992 and 0.949 → 0.996, the ring at 1 s 0.982 → 0.994, mesh-burst ×64 0.921 → 0.945 with 341 ± 49 s less airtime (30 seeds); no message dropped for want of a route in any of them, where main dropped 4 to 96 a run; the corridors' latency p90 2 to 5 s longer; the ring at 2 s 0.9967 → 0.9910 (−0.006 ± 0.005) from two seeds that hit the leaf-table failure of D39, which main shows in other seeds. Measured again against main `4ed5c0b`, with D41 in: S4 ×1 97.0 → 96.7 %, ×3 21.1 → **35.8 %** (seed 1); the ring at 1 s 0.989 → 0.995 (+0.006 ± 0.001) and at 2 s 0.9968 → 0.9981 (+0.0013 ± 0.0004, 30 seeds), so the ring's loss was D39's and not the rule's. | The collapse note found that at three times the spec "Capacity and sizing" rate the hand-over breaks first: attempts lost to busy and deaf receivers make hand-overs fail, each failure sends the message the long way round at once, and nothing slows the sender, so frames per text quadruple before any relay refuses. A refusal measures one hand-over's refill at the receiver; silence says only that the channel or the receiver is full, and not how full, so its ban doubles. The ban alone, with the message rerouted at once, changes nothing measurable: every message then takes the long way at once, which is the loop itself. Waiting for the same neighbour is what slows the sender, and it keeps what the throw-away experiment of the collapse note gained (whole-relay hold, rerun on the same seeds: 250 kHz ×3 88.0 %, ×4 27.7 %, 125 kHz ×3 30.0 %, ×4 4.9 %). Not solved by it: past the cliff messages still live 48 to 58 minutes in the network and the domain still collapses, at 250 kHz ×3 30 minutes later, so a message still needs an end by time (the TTL of the draft decision); a link that works one way only holds the messages that have no other way on for as long as its heartbeats are heard. Of refusals for room, those at a relay with a hop place free for another message the refused sender held for it were 17 % at ×2 and 63 to 96 % at ×3: a refusal naming the lowest count it takes would matter only past the cliff. Decided 2026-10-16. |
| D46 | **One node type with one setting, sleepy** (spec "Node identity", "Heartbeat", "Neighbour table", "Addresses", "Sleepy nodes", "Relay choice", "Beats", "Heard table", "Delivery", "Delivery queue", "A sleepy node's own message", "Sleepy nodes alone", "Packet formats"; `docs/one-node-type-2026-10-16.md`). Every node runs `Relay`, with a 4-byte node ID from its chip that names it in DATA and ACK; an address is a position and an ID; the heartbeat is 9 bytes, ID and short position, as long on air as 8. A sleepy node takes the strongest relay it hears as its anchor, and its home and address are the anchor's; it heartbeats on its home channel only, at a random time in the second half of each 300 s period (half the delivery TTL); a holder sends it what it holds as DATA in the 9.25 s after each beat, with the time left the message arrived with; it hands its own message to its anchor with count 0, retried a fade later; it is a next hop only where no relay is live. It sleeps only while it has an anchor: without one it listens all the time and acts as a relay. ATTACH, ATTACHED, DELIVER, the leaf table and the polls go, and a relay no longer freezes its position: a node's position is its GPS fix unless the fix is implausible or the app sets a map point. | *Measured* against main `8cd70a4`, S4 seeds 1 and 2, with holders sending on the beat (what a sleeping node needs) and, as the simulated nodes listen, at once: at the spec "Capacity and sizing" rate delivered 96.7 % on main, 93.2 % on the beat (−3.4 ± 0.1 points, latency p50 130 s against 1 s), 96.3 % at once (−0.4 ± 0.0); at twice it 96.0, 89.9 and 93.7 % (−2.2 ± 1.0 at once); at three times all collapse (37.7, 25.9, 32.9 %). A dark node is heard back after 114 s at the median against 58 s. Presence takes 212 ms an hour per sleepy node against 292 ms for a leaf's polls and answers, and 7 to 8 % of lost DATA had a sleepy beat on the air at its receiver against 15 % with a poll or its answer. Suites at once: as main, rings at their cap carrying up to 5 % less; on the beat, flows to one node faster than its window carries lose a third to a half. A sleepy node is a full node, 160 KiB of RAM against a leaf's 0.8 KiB, and listens 4 % of the time, 0.24 mA against 11 µA. Awake sleepy nodes alone, which the simulator never sleeps and which by the rule above do not sleep, deliver where main delivers nothing: a village of 60, all; a square kilometre of S4's 714, 98 %; a mesh of sleepers without relays was not measured. Three rules of the brief failed as given and now do what a leaf did: a sleepy node's own message to the neighbour nearest its destination collapsed the domain at twice the rate (62 and 36 %); sent as the origin of its search with count 1 it took hop places (the ring at its cap carried a third less); a node back from the dark took the first node it heard (anchor changes 20 000 against 8 200). *Re-measured* after the rebase onto `994e1ce` (D44), against it: at the rate 96.6 % on main, 93.1 % on the beat (−3.5 ± 0.1), 96.2 % at once (−0.4 ± 0.1); at twice it 95.7, 89.9 and 92.4 % (−3.4 ± 1.7 at once); the suites as before. Heartbeating as a relay until it has an anchor, every sleepy node of S4 booting at once doubles presence airtime over the run, 467 ms an hour per node. *Decided 2026-10-17: adopted.* Next step: a bit in DATA that says more follows, so that a node after its beat listens only briefly unless told more is coming, and holders that send a node that has just beaten its messages first. |
| D47 | **A sleepy node sleeps: the window after a beat, "more follows", and waking for the anchor's heartbeat** (spec "Heartbeat", "Neighbour table", "Channel moves", "Send queue", "Sleepy nodes", "Relay choice", "Beats", "Delivery", "Delivery queue", "Packet formats", "Implementation notes"; `core-interface.md` §3 rule 10; `docs/sleep-window-2026-10-17.md`). | D49 | **A heartbeat says whether its sender sleeps and whether it knows a relay; a sleepy node anchors on the first relay it hears** (spec "Heartbeat", "Neighbour table", "Next hop", "Channel at boot", "Relay choice", "Beats", "Sleepy nodes alone", "Packet formats"; `docs/sleepy-next-hop-2026-10-17.md`). (2) A sleepy node takes the first relay it hears as its anchor at once, stays awake a worst-case rotation and then takes the strongest it heard without the switching margin; it is anchorless only while it hears no relay. | D51 | **Anchorless mode: a sleepy node without an anchor receives duty-cycled and is reached with a long preamble** (spec "Physical layer", "Duty-cycled receive and the long preamble", "Regulatory limits", "Channel at boot", "Heartbeat", "Neighbour table", "Hand-over", "Retry wait", "Next hop", "Sleepy nodes", "Relay choice", "Beats", "Heard table", "Delivery", "Sleepy nodes alone", "Capacity and sizing", "Packet formats", "Implementation notes"; `core-interface.md` §3 rules 4, 8, 11; `docs/anchorless-2026-10-18.md`). The heartbeat's bits mean one thing each. Sleepy: reach me only in my window after my beat; that the anchor sends follows from the address. Anchorless: I am sleepy and know no node that is not; I hold and forward as a relay, send with long preambles and am reached at any time with one; send my mail to me directly; if you are not sleepy, wake me with a long preamble. The anchor is the address's position, the channel and the watched heartbeat; a holder is whoever holds a message for the node and hears its beat. A node in the mode receives duty-cycled (`Action::Sniff`): a 6.0 ms window every 361.1 ms, from the datasheet's two conditions (§13.1.7) and the cycle that balances the windows' current against its own beats, 0.194 mA; every frame to it, its beats and a relay's answers carry 1396 symbols of preamble, 357.4 ms. It beats once a minute, at a random time in the second half of the minute, on the next channel each time; a relay that hears a beat answers on the node's channel with a long heartbeat at once and a fade later; its entry expires after two of its rotations, 1591 s. A sleepy node listens two rotations at boot; a retry after a long frame waits longer by as much as the frame is; a heartbeat may lag, and a check be held, by a long full frame, 992.9 ms and 492.9 ms. The standard and loaded suites of D42 with sleeping nodes deliver as main; S4 ×1, seeds 1 and 2, +0.0005 ± 0.0001. Measured and not built: a sleeping node that follows its anchor to a new channel beats there, at once and again (F) or once at a random time within a heartbeat interval (G): S4 +0.0054 either, airtime +2.0 % (F) or +0.4 % (G); the loaded rings with sleeping nodes up to +0.015 (F), within their spread (G). | The ring at its cap, its sleepy nodes listening, jammed on some seeds whatever the variant and the start: a backlog of up to 361 texts in flight at the end, none dropped. The ring is at its cap on every seed (each relay is offered 10.74 % of airtime against the bucket's 9.83 %, carries 2.80 texts a second where its measured cost per text allows 2.76, and its origins hold back 28 % of the texts), but that is pushback working and not the jam. A jam is a holder that stops delivering to a node in reach: it missed one beat in five while transmitting or receiving at its cap, forgot a node that was taking a text every 2 s once the beats of a delivery TTL went unheard (on half the seeds), or, after its own channel move, sent on the old channel to a node that had followed it. The node's messages filled the delivery queue, were refused, and waited at the relays before it, which, at their cap, could not drain them before the run ended. Both rules matter only to a node reached outside the windows its beats open; a sleeping node's holder renews the entry and learns the channel with each beat it hears. Not addressed: a relay's own move reaches the neighbours that send to it only with its next heartbeat on their channel, up to a rotation. *Decided 2026-10-18: adopted.* |
| D54 | **A sleepy node backs off its silent anchor, and a refusal says how long to wait, back to the application** (spec "Addresses", "Bans", "Receiving DATA", "A sleepy node's own message", "Packet formats"; `core-interface.md` §4, §7; `relay/forward.rs`, `tables/budget.rs`, `tables/records.rs`, `wire.rs`; `docs/collapse-source-2026-10-18.md`). Two rules, adopted 2026-10-19 on main `1419f25`. (1) A sleepy node whose own message goes unanswered tries its anchor again a fade later doubled for each silence in a row, as the ban after silence doubles. (2) A refusal is a NACK, type 0x7, 14 bytes at the ACK's 14.4 ms, whose 14th byte asks for a wait in whole bans of 1.56 s, rounded down, up to 255 (398 s; a longer wait is told as that): the places in a refuser's line are a ban apart and nothing acts sooner than a ban; for want of room the wait is the bucket's time to hold the queue, and the refused senders stand in line one ban apart; a relay refuses fresh work while the neighbour it would go to is banned and asks for the rest of that ban; the sender bans the refuser for the wait; a sleepy node gives its refused message back to its application with the wait. Found on the way and adopted meanwhile as D55 and D56: the home busy reading as any other channel's, and a delivery that needs the bucket for itself only. Measured and not kept: refusing forwards too for the way on (the pushback hop by hop), carrying the way on's ban in the wait told to a forward refused for want of room (the middle form, reverted), the wait in units of 2.5 s, the told wait on three spare `aux` bits, the told wait in place of the sender's ladder, an age signal for the application (not built: a node sees two texts an hour), a share per sender (not built: senders differ by cost, not rate). | The slide of S4 at twice the texting rate in every version measured, and the strip's collapse at the spec rate, were the channel-move rule fed by a home reading that counted others' frames twice; past the cap the losses are at the ends: texts aged at their sender after 27 offers each, and texts that reached a holder at its cap and expired in its queue. *Measured* on main `1419f25`, S4 at three times the rate, two settled hours, seed 1 (seed 2): delivered 55.3 (54.5) % on main, 66.7 % with (1), 77.7 (72.6) % with (1) and (2) in bans, 77.2 % in units of 2.5 s, 76.7 (70.8) % with the middle form too; on main, with (1), and with (1) and (2) in units of 2.5 s, seed 1: aged in transit 1.4, 0.7, 0.1 %; aged at the origin 33.4, 23.4, 5.6 %, and refused at it 0, 0, 7.5 %; delivery queues 9.2, 8.8, 9.3 %; the texts refused or aged at their origin take 29.4, 18.0 and 10.0 % of the texts' airtime; latency p50 174, 109, 85 s; airtime −16 and −18 %. Refusing forwards too: 3.2 % delivered, the loaded ring −57 ± 4 points. With (1) and (2) in bans, S4 at the spec rate 94.0 → 93.3 %, at twice it 93.0 → 92.2 % (the cost is (1)'s: texts aged at their sender 0.5 → 1.2 %), the strip at the spec rate 92.5 → 92.4 % (in units of 2.5 s), 92.0 % in bans with the middle form. The told waits run to 63 bans, longer the farther from the origin a forward is refused, which the middle form did not turn into more delivered. Forwards are a quarter of the refusals, being taken into hop places whatever the bucket holds. The suites against main: S2 −0.8 ± 0.3 (1), the corridor at 1 s −2.2 ± 0.5, at 0.5 s −3.1 ± 1.1, the ring at 1 s −2.0 ± 0.7 (2), the rest within their spread; the middle form showed no herd in them. On main `8704790` and `4ccbaf4` D55 and D56 took S4 at twice the rate from 61, 42 % to 90, 90 % and the strip at the spec rate from 25 to 92 %. The delivery queue already keeps a text a full TTL from its arrival; under (1) and (2) at three times the rate 9.2 % of the texts expire there, 8.8 to 8.9 % for nodes their holder never heard while the text waited, with 57 and 160 refusals for a full queue in two hours: a longer stay would not be collected. The rise of that loss from 5.1 % at the spec rate to 8.8 % at three times it follows the anchors' beat reception, 76 → 62 %, lost to the anchor's own load (receiving another frame 5 → 14 %, transmitting 2 → 7 %), not to weak links (13 % both). *Decided 2026-10-19: (1) and (2) adopted; the middle form, the 2.5 s unit and the rest measured are not.* |
| D55 | **What breaks first at the edge of capacity is the channel move rule: the home channel is read as any other, and a delivery in its window needs room in the bucket for itself only** (spec "Busy estimate", "Duty cycle cap", "Send queue", "Delivery queue"; `tables/busy.rs`, `node/relay.rs` `sweep_sample`, `set_aside_own`, `node/relay/delivery.rs` `try_deliver`, `node/relay/forward.rs` `no_room_to_deliver`; `docs/collapse-network-2026-10-18.md`). (1) A home reading is the same 0/1 RSSI reading as a reading of any other channel, set aside while a packet that follows the relay is on the air; the receive time of packets decoded there no longer counts (revises the home reading of D24). The trace: at twice the spec "Capacity and sizing" rate the home channel read 7 to 12 % busy against 2 to 4 % for the quietest, half the relays were over the move threshold, and the domain's relays moved 1 191 and 1 161 times in two hours; frames went to channels their receiver had left, failed hand-overs split messages (in 43 % the receiver had it), the copies searched on, and the frames raised the readings. Read alike (1): moves 9 and 6; ×2 delivered 92.7 and 93.1 % flat in every ten minutes against 71.4 % sliding to 43 % and 89.1 %, at 70 % of the airtime; ×1 93.9 and 93.9 % against 93.9 and 93.8 %, moves 0 against 131 and 88; the strip at the spec rate 91.5 % against 24.6 %, its middle domain at the cap 5 % of the time against 81 %, the middle's own texts 88.6 % against 4.1 %; at half the rate 94.3 % against 94.0 %. All 13 channels carry the centre's relays; the per-relay cap binds, not a channel's airtime (4 to 6 % busy). With (2) as well, at three times the rate 54.8 and 52.8 % against 33.4 and 31.2 %, 7.8 and 7.4 texts a second against 9.1 at twice the rate, deliveries expired in queues 8 400 against 27 700; ×2 and ×1 unchanged. D42 suites, (1): within the spread; (1) and (2): the rings +5 points, mesh-burst ×64 +18, the corridor at 0.5 s −1.3 ± 0.6. Measured on `8704790` and not proposed: no moves at all (as (1)); a move announced on all 13 channels (stops the slide, 1.4 points lower); a copy ended where the message has been (stops the slide, loses messages without a trace, rejected); a refused message waiting for its refuser (collapses sooner, rejected). A lower hop cap would save at most a twentieth of the airtime; relay density not measured. *Verified 2026-10-19 on D56's late-interferer scenario, seven interferers switched on at 3600 s in a settled S4, seed 1: relays within 600 m of one on its channel 7 → 2 by the end, as with D56's form and against 7 → 7 without moves; 94.0 % delivered. Decided 2026-10-19: adopted, both.* |
| D56 | **The slide at twice the rate is the move rule reacting to the network's own load; a running node's home estimate counts no frame of the network, and a delivery needs the bucket for itself alone** (spec "Busy estimate", "Delivery queue"; `node/relay.rs` `count_busy`, `node/relay/delivery.rs` `try_deliver`; `docs/collapse-wild-2026-10-18.md`). (1) Once running, a node counts only foreign energy at home: a reading inside any frame of the network is set aside, and no frame counts toward its busy time; booting, it counts every frame, so that its choice spreads the load. This widens D24. (2) A delivery in its node's window needs a place in the regular room and its own frame and the reply reserve in the bucket; what is queued behind it does not count, since it goes first. Measured and not built: dropping the oldest queued forward when the regular room is full (against D31), six channels of 250 kHz (against D43), a receiver that stops receiving a frame for another node after its first five bytes (a bench question), quiet slots around heartbeats (at most 1.7 % of attempts; D60). | *Measured* S4 for two hours of load, seeds 1 and 2. Against main `8704790`: at twice the spec "Capacity and sizing" rate relays moved after their boot choice 1 492 and 1 660 times a run against 150 and 162 at the rate, every relay about every half hour, and sleepy nodes spent 8.0 and 8.7 % of their time on a channel their anchor had left; with moves off, or with (1), twice the rate delivered 89.9 % in both seeds, flat, against 63.7 and 42.5 %, with half the airtime and the busiest relay at 7.5 %; relays still left a channel an interferer switched on at 3600 s (7 → 2 within 600 m) with 5 moves instead of 170. (2) alone 71.3 and 53.6 %; on top of (1) at three times the rate 56.2 and 52.4 % against 32.7 and 27.6 %; mesh-burst ×64 0.648 → 0.909, the rings +4 to 5 points. Against main `4ccbaf4` (D58): ×1 94.0 and 93.7 against 93.9 and 93.8 %; ×2 93.0 and 92.9 against 71.4 and 89.1 %; ×3 51.5 and 50.9 against 14.5 and 12.0 %; the strip, one seed, at the rate 88.6 against 24.6 % (sliding from 90 to 84 % in its last 20 minutes, the middle domain's relays at their cap 11 % of the time against 54 %), at half the rate 94.3 against 94.0 %; suites: the rings +4.8 to 5.7, mesh-burst ×64 +18.2, the corridor at 0.5 s −1.3 ± 0.5 points, all in texts still under way at the end. Dropping the oldest at three times the rate, on top of both: 82.4 % against 51.5 % (seed 1, `4ccbaf4`), 85.5 and 85.3 % against 56.2 and 52.4 % (`8704790`). Six channels of 250 kHz on top of both: 89.9 and 89.6 % at three times the rate on `8704790`, 35.2 % on `4ccbaf4`. *Decided 2026-10-19: the cause understood, the same as D55 found. (1) superseded by D55's form, which reads the home channel as any other channel and is adopted; (2) adopted as D55's rule, the same; the simulator's count of messages sharing an ID kept; nothing else adopted.* |
| D57 | **A node that follows its anchor to a new channel beats there within a heartbeat interval; random IDs do not raise the DATA sent to sleeping nodes** (spec "Channel moves", "Beats"; `node/relay.rs` `reconsider_anchor`; `docs/small-items-2026-10-18.md`). (1) A sleeping node that follows its anchor to a new home channel brings its next beat forward to a random time within a heartbeat interval, 0–15 s, if that is sooner, so its holder learns where it listens without waiting up to a beat period, and the nodes that follow on one heartbeat do not beat together (D53's G). No wire change, no new constant. (2) No change for the DATA to sleeping nodes from others: it is the stale anchorless entry of D51. | *Measured* against main `8704790`. F (beat at once and again soon) was not measured again. (2) Traced over twelve runs of D52's models B and I: 99.9 % of the DATA that reaches a sleeping node from a node not its anchor is a hand-over, with the long preamble, from a node in anchorless mode to a neighbour it keeps as anchorless, which has since found an anchor and beats only on its anchor's channel; the entry was last renewed 1 035 s before at the median, mostly by the node's beats in an anchorless spell of 10 to 60 minutes. A few pockets at the edge of relay coverage make the count, 0 to 846 a run; random IDs change it by +16 ± 49 over six seeds on main and +136 ± 200 on D52's code, where the two seeds D52 ran both rose. Built, measured and removed: forgetting an anchorless neighbour once two failed hand-overs demote it, which halves DATA with the long preamble (5 to 7 % of S4's airtime) but delivers 2.25 ± 0.74 points less in the village beside a district of D51 and 0.19 ± 0.22 less in S4. *Decided 2026-10-18: item 1 no change, item 2 adopted.* |
| D58 | **Every sleepy node receives duty-cycled between its windows, is a last-resort next hop reached with the long preamble, and holds no mail** (spec "Duty-cycled receive and the long preamble", "Neighbour table", "Next hop", "Sleepy nodes", "Relay choice", "Beats", "Heard table", "Delivery", "Delivery queue", "Sleepy nodes alone", "Packet formats"; `core-interface.md` §3 rule 10). The sleepy bit means "reach me cheaply in my window after my beat, or at any time with the long preamble"; the anchorless bit means only "I know no node that is not sleepy; if you are one, tell me", and is set with the sleepy bit (0xB; 0xA is no heartbeat). A sleepy neighbour with an anchor is a next hop only where no other candidate is live, with the long preamble, so a pocket of anchorless nodes behind a node that anchored keeps its way out; a sleepy node with anchorless neighbours beats once a period on each of their channels with the long preamble, so they learn the way. To measure with the implementation: delivered, sleepy-node current, DATA sent into nodes that cannot hear, long-frame airtime, and the RAM saved on the sleepy build; separately, a holder delivering outside the window with a long frame, as a latency lever. *Measured* against the old code (`sleepy-reach-2026-10-18.md`), first as decided, with the review's four points (last resort only where the search would end here, anchorless period 600 / 13 s, its entries 26 periods, a long copy of each beat): S4 seeds 1 and 2 delivered +0.40 ± 0.25 points, latency unchanged, sleepy-node current 0.399 → 0.593 mA (+0.194), DATA into nodes that cannot hear 741 → 15 a run (the 15 at a window's end), long-preamble airtime 4 585 → 30 857 s and all airtime 33 011 → 59 454 s, of which 26 125 s are the long beats to anchorless neighbours, sent by a quarter of the sleepy nodes after catching one anchorless beat; the village beside a district +1.2 ± 1.2 points, DATA into deaf nodes 412 → 1, airtime +1 697 s, village current +0.52 mA, twice the village nodes carrying others' texts at the same current; the village of sixty 100 % as before, at 0.702 mA against 0.590 and 2 763 s of airtime against 2 154 from the shorter period, its 38 s an hour of beats as derived; the D42 suites within their spread but the corridor at 0.5 s, −1.4 ± 0.7, from a few seeds whose end relay misses its neighbour through the boot; the sleepy image 19 000 B less RAM (155 372 B) and 22 408 B less flash. *Amended 2026-10-18 by the user* after these results: the long beats go to an anchorless neighbour only once it has been heard anchorless in two consecutive rotations of its beats (300 to 601 s apart), since one heard once is most often switching on; | D59 | **Seven rules the simulator had never exercised, measured** (`docs/unproven-2026-10-18.md`; `docs/simulator-design.md` §3.7, §3.8; `scenarios/dark-groups.toml`, `scenarios/s3-interferer.toml`). The simulator gains pairs of nodes forced to share an ID, groups of sleepy nodes that go dark together, sleepy nodes that ride in vehicles, interferers that reach walkers where they are and may appear later, GPS faults with the driver's plausibility checks, and nodes held on one channel; each is off by default. (1) Shared IDs (spec "Node identity"): a pair costs itself, not the domain: S4 with ten pairs of each of six kinds −0.3 points; in a district, two sleepy nodes in reach of each other lose a quarter of their mail to each other (69.9 against 92.5 %), a relay pair's sleepy nodes 2 to 4 points; and a sleepy node whose anchor shares its ID with a sleepy node in reach stopped its core (D64). (2) Group dark spells (spec "Delivery queue"): at S4's density the 41 places never bind (at most 17 held) and the TTL decides, a 20-minute spell losing half the group's mail whatever the queue; where a relay holds for more than 256 nodes its heard table binds first and starves the nodes it has no place for (D61). (3) Vehicles (spec "Relay choice"): riders do not keep up, but the delivery queue does not save their mail (texts to a rider being driven 6 to 7 % delivered, at a stop under 60 %, against 92 % for the same nodes static), and they do not wait for arrival: seven anchor checks an hour, six times a static node's current; checking on arrival saves a sixth of that and costs mail, not proposed (D65). (4) The answer heartbeat (spec "Heartbeat"): one answer costs each sleeping node on its channel one check, as the spec says, but an anchorless node that does not hear its relay is answered for as long as it stays, and each sleeping node there checks seven times an hour at three times its current (D62); in S4 the case is rare, checks −0.6 % with D62. (5) GPS checks (spec "Position"): they refuse every jump, but a drift of under 50 m a fix passes the jump check fix by fix and took a relay's mail down by a third within the hour; a tenth of S4's relays so pulled cost the whole domain 17 to 20 points (D63); the C/N0 check cannot be modelled. (6) Interferers with walkers: combinable; the move rule behaves as without walkers, the relays on the new transmitter's channel moving off it once each, none back. (7) Quiet slots: D60. | The review found these rules argued in the spec and never run. Each was measured where it bites, on S4 where the domain mattered and on the district of `dark-groups.toml` (four square kilometres at S4's densities) or a laboratory of a few nodes where it did not. Four rules hold as stated: shared IDs matter to their pair (with D64's loop the exception), one answer costs one check, the jump check, and the move rule with walkers. Three are wrong in a way a fix addresses, each proposed on a branch of its own and not merged: D61, D62 and D63; D64 fixes a loop a shared ID uncovered; the vehicle sentence is wrong and the fix tried for it (D65) does not pay; the quiet slots (D60) do nothing. Not done: a domain-wide spoofer that moves every receiver to one place, vehicles stopping briefly, and D58's path for item 1. *Decided 2026-10-19: the simulator models adopted; D61 to D64 adopted, D60 and D65 not; the vehicle sentence of spec "Relay choice" now says that mail to a node that leaves its holder's reach is lost unless the sender learns the new address.* |

Few heartbeats are lost to frames, and those mostly to senders that do not know the heartbeat's sender: a node can keep quiet only around its own neighbours' slots. Nothing to tell from the spread in the design's domain; the code is not kept. *Decided 2026-10-19: not adopted.* |

S4 as it is: 91.06 → 90.78 and 90.44 → 90.84 %, anchor checks −15 %, current 0.396 → 0.380 and 0.402 → 0.384 mA. S2 with every node walking falls below its scenario test's bound, 55.8 % against 60 %. It trades mail for current; the mail riders lose waits out its TTL in the old holder's queue, which no rule of the node's own changes. Spec "Relay choice" should say that: mail to a node that moves out of its holder's reach is lost unless its sender learns the new address. *Decided 2026-10-19: not adopted; the spec sentence changed as the note says.* |
| D66 | **A stale text stays queued until its place is needed** (spec "Delivery queue", "Receiving DATA", "Owner", "Bounds", "Sleepy nodes", "Relay choice"; `tables/delivery.rs`, `relay/delivery.rs`, `relay/forward.rs`; `docs/mailbox-anchor-load-2026-10-19.md`). Adopted 2026-10-19, on main `c0572cb`. A text queued in a delivery queue the delivery TTL ago or longer is stale: it stays, and goes to its node at the node's next beat as any other. When a text arrives at a full queue, the oldest stale text makes way; a queue full of texts that are not stale refuses with a wait as now, so pushback is unchanged. The DATA to the node carries `time_left` as the text had it when it reached the holder, as now, never below zero: the destination learns the time on the way, not the time in the queue. No wire change. D44's bound of two TTLs on a life no longer holds; place bounds the stay in the queue. Measured and not kept, each built on main alone and reverted: (a) a sleepy node's beats on a fixed interval from its ID, half to one beat period, a protocol change (beats are random on main), with the holder sending in the windows of beats it missed; (b) the long preamble after missed beats, not built; (c) a sleeping node leaving an anchor it heard in fewer than half of eight watches; (d) the first choice waiting a rotation more for a relay heard twice with margin; a relay telling a node its beats arrive under its margin, a bit in the ACK, not built. | The texts that expire in a holder's queue for a node it never heard while they waited are of three kinds: nodes at the edge of coverage, whose beats reach the holder below sensitivity, nodes dark the whole wait, and nodes whose beats the busy holder misses. The rise with load is the anchors' own load: with the sleepy nodes at the spec rate and the relays busier by their own texts, beats received fall 76 → 68 % and texts expiring unheard rise 5.06 → 6.44 %. The edge nodes have no better relay: their link to the holder is −123 dBm at the median, their strongest relay 1.5 dB better, 5 % have a relay with margin, and half are walkers, so no rule of anchor choice helps them. Keeping a stale text loses nothing that a newer text needs, and its node may beat yet, back from the dark or heard in a fade. *Measured* against main `c0572cb`, S4, two settled hours, seed 1 (seed 2): texts expiring unheard ×1 5.06 (5.06) %, of them below sensitivity 2.78, dark 1.73, the holder busy 0.40 %; ×3 8.83 (8.88) %: 4.38, 1.86, 2.28 %. Stale texts kept: delivered ×1 93.28 → 95.99 (93.30 → 96.42) %, ×3 77.72 → 81.56 (72.62 → 77.46) %; delivered past the TTL ×1 2.79 (3.05) %, ×3 5.52 (5.72) %, their time in the queue p50 850–872 s, p90 1636–1988 s, at most 7571 s; queues hold four to five times what they did at ×1 and fill at the busiest relays at ×3, refusals for a full queue at ×3 57 → 29 (160 → 134); airtime ×1 +3.4 (−0.2) %, ×3 +1.6 (+1.0) %; at ×3 refused at the origin +0.8 (+0.5), aged there +0.5 (+0.1) points. (a): texts expiring unheard at ×3 8.83 → 6.44 (8.88 → 6.22) %, but delivered 77.72 → 77.25 (72.62 → 72.38) %, refused at the origin +2.2 (+2.3) points, unanswered deliveries doubled; ×1 +0.14 point; the heard table +2 KiB. (c): ×1 93.28 → 93.10 %, ×3 77.72 → 76.01 (72.62 → 72.41) %, anchor changes +18 (+16) %, current +4 %. (d): ×1 93.23 %, ×3 74.29 (73.28) %, no effect beyond the spread; (c) and (d) together ×1 93.25 %, ×3 76.25 %. *Decided 2026-10-19: the stale text kept is adopted; (a), (c) and (d) are not.* |
| D67 | **A message without an end by time on its way: measured, the time left kept** (spec section "Time to live" (before D113), spec "Overload waits", "Bounds"; `relay/forward.rs` `receive_data`, `expire`, `transmit_current`, `time_gone_at`; `docs/no-transit-ttl-2026-10-19.md`). Asked: whether DATA's time left (D44) is still needed now that overload goes back to the application (D54, D66). Measured: the receive check, the send-queue sweep and the last attempt end a message by time only at its origin, before a first hop takes it; the wire and the stamping unchanged. Reverted on the branch, with its spec text; the simulator's read-only record instruments kept.
